Practical algorithm:
- Work with probabilities p_i (nonnegative sum1).
- Compute convolution c = fft(p,p).
- Objective M=max c.
- Subgradient: pick active set A (top few indices), g = (2/|A|) Σ_{k∈A} (P_k p) where (P_k p)_i = p_{k-i} (valid). Then update p ← normalize( p - η g ), maybe with momentum. Ensure p≥0.
- Since objective nonconvex, use random restarts/perturbations.
- Could also use "active set growth" to reduce all high coefficients.

LP implementation details in Python:
```
def build_A(p, n):
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32)
    # total n*n
    rows = np.empty(n*n, dtype=np.int32)
    cols = np.empty(n*n, dtype=np.int32)
    data = np.empty(n*n, dtype=np.float64)
    idx=0
    for i in range(n):
        rows[idx:idx+n] = i + ar
        cols[idx:idx+n] = ar
        data[idx:idx+n] = p[i]
        idx += n
    return scipy.sparse.coo_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1, n)).tocsr()
```
But rows = i+ar for i up to n-1 gives max 2n-2, ok. CoO sorted? linprog may sort. Building each iteration O(n²) and loops. For n=512, 512*512=262k assignments, trivial. For 512 iterations 134M, okay maybe seconds in Python loops? The loop 512 with vector slice assignments per iteration (not per element) fast.
Could precompute rows and cols once! The positions (i+j,j) independent of p. Data changes with p. Precompute rows, cols arrays of length n*n. Then in each iteration data = np.tile(p, n)?? Need ordering. If rows/cols fill in i-major (for each i, j=0..n-1), data should be p[i] repeated n times. We can do data = np.repeat(p, n) if order i-major. Precompute rows/cols once.

cols=Cols_j.ravel().astype(np.int32)
```

Let's design softmax Adam fallback in detail.

Softmax Adam function:
```
def optimize_softmax(p0, n, max_time, log=True):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(...)
    p = p0.copy(); p=np.clip(p,1e-300,None); p/=p.sum()
    x = np.log(p+1e-300); x -= x[-1]
    # freeze last? We'll keep full x but after each step set x[-1]=0? Softmax invariant; to reduce dimension, set x = x - x[-1].
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    tau_factor=5.0 # will decay to 0.2
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score(p)
    lr=0.05
    while time+max_time:
       c=conv(p); M=c.max(); score=M*2n
       if score<best_score: update
       tau = max(1e-12, tau_factor * max(M, 1/(2*n-1)))
       # weights
       shifted=(c-M)/tau
       # clip shifted to [-20,0]
       w=np.exp(np.clip(shifted,-30,0)); s=w.sum(); if s<=0: w uniform? 
       w/=s
       # g_p = 2 corr(p,w): using FFT length L>=2n-1
       L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
       Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L); Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
       gp=2*np.fft.irfft(np.conj(Fp)*Fw,L)[:n].real
       # softmax gradient: gx = p*(gp - p.dot(gp))
       gx = p*(gp - np.dot(p,gp))
       # set last coordinate not updated? We'll update all and then x-=x[-1]; Adam on n-1 maybe
       t+=1
       m = bet1*m+(1-bet1)*gx
       v = bet2*v+(1-bet2)*gx*gx
       mhat=m/(1-bet1**t); vhat=v/(1-bet2**t)
       # use only first n-1, set last 0
       dx = -lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
       dx[-1]=0
       x += dx
       x = x - x[-1]
       # clip x to avoid extreme
       x = np.clip(x, -50, 50)
       p = np.exp(x - x.max()); p /= p.sum()
       # adapt tau_factor
       tau_factor = max(0.2, 5.0 * 0.995**t)
       # adaptive lr? maybe lr = 0.5/n? but Adam dimensionless.
```

def convolve_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
    if n <= 1024: return np.convolve(a,a)
    L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length(); F=np.fft.rfft(a,L); c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)[:2*n-1]; return c
```

def precompute_A_positions(n):
    ar=np.arange(n,dtype=np.int32)
    Ri, Gj = np.meshgrid(ar, ar, indexing='ij')
    rows=(Ri+Gj).ravel().astype(np.int32)
    cols=Gj.ravel().astype(np.int32)
    return rows, cols
```
For n=512 memory ok. But Ri/Gj arrays 262k each. Use np.ravel.

```
def solve_lp_direction(p, n, rows, cols, M, time_limit=5.0):
    data=np.repeat(p, n)
    A=scipy.sparse.coo_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    A=A.tocsr()
    c=np.full(n,-1.0)
    b=np.full(2*n-1,M)
    bounds=[(0.0, None)]*n
    res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit':time_limit})
    if res is None or res.status not in (0,3): return None
    g=np.asarray(res.x,float)
    g=np.maximum(g,0)
    s=g.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return None
    g/=s
    return g
```
Status 3 is time limit? HiGHS status: 0 optimal, 1 infeasible, 2 unbounded, 3 time limit, 4 numeric? We can accept if x available? Maybe only 0.
The b_ub M is small (~1/n). Objective max sum; if p positive, optimal sum maybe ~1. Fine.

Line search:
```
def line_search(p,g,score_current,n):
    best_p=p; best_s=score_current
    alphas=[0.1,0.2,0.05,0.3,0.5,0.02,1.0]
    for alpha in alphas:
       p_t=(1-alpha)*p+alpha*g
       if p_t.sum()<=0: continue
       p_t/=p_t.sum()
       s=score_norm(p_t)
       if s<best_s:
          best_s=s; best_alpha=alpha
    return best_p,best_s,best_alpha
```
Need include alpha=0? no.

LP iterate:
```
def lp_optimize(p0, n, rows, cols, deadline, max_iters=None):
    p=p0.copy(); p=np.clip(p,1e-12,None); p/=p.sum()
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_norm(p)
    no_improve=0
    i=0
    while time < deadline and no_improve<20:
       c=convolve_fast(p); M=c.max()
       score=2*n*M
       # If M too low close to bound, stop
       if score < 2*n/(2*n-1)*(1+1e-4): break
       # Solve with current M
       g=solve_lp_direction(...)
       if g is None: break
       p_new,s_new,alpha=line_search(p,g,best_score,n)
       if s_new < best_score*(1-1e-8):
           p=p_new; best=p.copy(); best_score=s_new; no_improve=0; print
       else:
           # try tighter M targets
           improved=False
           for lam in [0.98,0.95,0.9]:
              M2=M*lam
              g2=solve_lp_direction(... M2 ...)
              if g2 is None: continue
              p2,s2,alpha2=line_search(...)
              if s2 < best_score*(1-1e-8): update; improved=True; break
           if not improved:
              # perturb p slightly and continue
              rng noise; p = normalize(p + noise*scale)
              # if perturbed score worse, keep p? Maybe use best
              no_improve+=1
       i+=1
    return best,best_score
```

Fallback local perturbation:
```
def random_polish(p_best, deadline, rng):
   p=p_best.copy(); score=...
   while time<deadline:
     # choose block width, multiply by exp(normal)
     cand=p.copy()
     width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//50), n//5+1))
     start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
     cand[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,size=width))
     cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); cand/=cand.sum()
     s=score_norm(cand)
     if s<score: p=cand; score=s; print
     else: occasionally symmetric? 
   return p,score
```

Softmax Adam could be more extensive. Maybe include if time. But code complexity high. We can implement a simpler gradient step using LSE top weights with multiplicative natural gradient, not full Adam. It might be easier and faster. Let's design a robust multiplicative update:
Given p, compute weights w over c (LSE or top). Compute g_i = 2 Σ_j w_{i+j} p_j (correlation). This is gradient of smooth loss wrt p. We want decrease loss: update p_i ← p_i * exp(-η g_i) (because gradient positive at peaks). This is mirror descent. Normalize. Choose η small. This avoids softmax variable and nonnegativity.
Pseudo:
```
def mirror_optimize(p, deadline):
  p=best; best_score=score
  tau_factor=5.0; eta=0.5
  while time:
    c=conv(p); M=c.max()
    if score<best: update
    tau=tau_factor*max(M, avg)
    w=exp((c-M)/tau); w/=w.sum()
    gp=2*correlation(p,w)
    # scale eta so max log update ~ step_size e.g. 0.1
    maxd=np.max(gp)
    if maxd>0:
       step=0.1 # relative log change
       eta=step/maxd
    p_new=p*np.exp(-eta*gp)
    p_new=np.clip(p_new,0,None); p_new/=p_new.sum()
    # optionally if score worse, reduce step and retry? 
    if score(p_new)<best_score: p=p_new; best=p_new; tau_factor=max(0.2,tau_factor*0.999)
    else:
       # try smaller eta (eta/3) maybe; if none, perturb
    ...
```
This is simpler and may work. The update p_i *= exp(-η g_i) reduces p at positions involved in active peaks. Since g_i is weighted sum of paired p over active k. For uniform, gp? c triangle, weights maybe top center, gp constant? For p uniform and w center? correlation of uniform with center weights: w_k for k near center, gp_i =2Σ_j w_{i+j}/n. For interior i, sum of w over a window length n maybe constant? If w concentrated center, gp_i ~2w_center/n constant for interior, edges smaller. It may push edges down? Not sure. But from asymmetric seed, may shape.

We can implement both mirror and LP; use time budget. Maybe LP stronger.

Could also upsample by repeating? Not good. Linear interpolation:
```
def upsample(p, n_new):
    x_old=np.arange(len(p))
    y=p
    x_new=np.arange(n_new) # maybe centered: (x_new+0.5)*len(p)/n_new -0.5
    # Use np.interp with left/right 0
    x_eval = (np.arange(n_new)+0.5) * (len(p)-1) / n_new
    p_new=np.interp(x_eval, x_old, y)
    p_new=np.maximum(p_new,0); p_new/=p_new.sum(); return p_new
```
This approximates density at uniform grid. If p has singular at 0, interpolation may reduce peak at first point? It samples at 0.5/old? For old index 0 value maybe high; new x_eval[0]=(0.5)*(N-1)/n_new small, between old 0 and1, lower. Sum normalized. Good.
Then run a few LP iterations at long? Maybe too heavy; instead mirror polish.

But final length larger increases lower bound closer to1, so any continuous score improves. For power law, score approaches 1.5708 from above? Interpolating 512 to 2048 likely lower. If 512 optimized 1.51, long maybe 1.50. Good.

p=np.asarray(p,float)

s=p.sum()
if s<=0: return None
return p/s
```

i=np.arange(n)+0.5
log1 = a*np.log(i)
log2 = b*np.log(i) + v  # v is log weight for second term (can be negative)
# If v very negative, second term negligible.
m=np.maximum(log1, log2)
val1=np.exp(log1-m)*1.0
val2=np.exp(log2-m)
p=val1+val2
p=np.maximum(p,0); p/=p.sum(); return p
```

Pseudo main:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+budget_s-20
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=512
    # initial grid
    candidates = generate_candidates(n, deadline maybe 60s)
    best_p,best_score = best
    # If global initial has better, include
    # Precompute LP positions if using
    rows, cols = precompute_positions(n) if scipy available and time before deadline-600
    # LP optimize best for maybe remaining*0.5
    if time<deadline-300:
       p2,s2 = lp_optimize(best_p, n, rows, cols, deadline-300)
       if s2<best_score: update
    # Mirror polish from best for remaining*0.4
    if time<deadline-60:
       p3,s3 = mirror_optimize(best_p, n, deadline-60)
       update
    # Random perturbation polish for remaining*0.2
    if time<deadline-30:
       p4,s4 = random_polish(best_p, n, deadline-30, rng)
       update
    # Upsample candidate(final)
    for nl in [1024,2048,512]: maybe if time
       pu=upsample(best_p, nl); su=score(pu); if finite and su<best_score: best=pu...
       # Also try mirror polish at nl? maybe not
    # Final validate with evaluate_sequence if available? 
    try:
       sc=evaluate_sequence(best.tolist())
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score: best_score=sc
    except Exception: pass
    return best.tolist()
```
Need ensure if scipy unavailable, skip LP.

Mirror optimize details and robust step:
```
def mirror_optimize(p0, n, deadline):
    p=normalize(p0)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score(p)
    tau_factor=5.0
    step_size=0.05 # max relative log update
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time<deadline and no_improve<300:
       c=convolve_fast(p); M=c.max(); score=2*n*M
       if score<best_score:
          best=p.copy(); best_score=score; no_improve=0; print
          # anneal
          tau_factor=max(0.2, tau_factor*0.995)
          step_size=max(0.01, step_size*0.999)
       else:
          no_improve+=1
       # compute weights
       avg=1.0/(2*n-1)
       tau=tau_factor*max(M, avg)
       # if tau too small, use top weights
       shifted=(c-M)/tau
       shifted=np.clip(shifted,-20,0)
       w=np.exp(shifted); sw=w.sum()
       if sw<=0 or not finite:
          w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[np.argmax(c)]=1
       else: w/=sw
       # correlation gp
       L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
       Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L); Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
       gp=2.0*np.fft.irfft(np.conj(Fp)*Fw,L)[:n].real
       gp=np.maximum(gp,0)
       maxd=gp.max()
       if maxd>0:
           eta=step_size/maxd
       else: break
       # maybe make update less extreme by using gp^0.7? Could try
       update=eta*gp
       # To avoid too large, clip update to step_size*2
       update=np.clip(update,0,step_size*2)
       p_new=p*np.exp(-update)
       p_new=normalize(p_new)
       s_new=score(p_new)
       if s_new < best_score*(1-1e-9):
           p=p_new
       else:
           # try half, quarter
           accepted=False
           for frac in [0.5,0.25,0.125]:
              p_try=p*np.exp(-update*frac)
              try norm
              s_try=score(...)
              if s_try < best_score*(1-1e-9): p=p_try; accepted=True; break
           if not accepted:
              # perturb p a bit? or keep
              if no_improve > 50:
                 # random noise
                 p=p*(1+0.05*rng.normal(size=n)); p=normalize(p)
                 if score(p)>best_score: p=best.copy()
       it+=1
    return best,best_score
```
Issue: Mirror update p*=exp(-eta gp) can push some p to zero, then score may be undefined if sum? Normalize. If p becomes too concentrated, score worse; line search half retries.
This is a bit ad hoc but may polish.

We could instead use Adam softmax as more stable. Mirror may get stuck with zero. But from nonnegative, okay.

def lp_optimize(...):
   it=0; no_imp=0
   while time<deadline and no_imp<10:
      c=conv(p); M=c.max()
      # solve maxsum
      g1=solve_maxsum(... time_limit=min(10, remaining/10))
      if g1: p_c, s_c = line_search(p,g1); if improve update; it++; no_imp=0; continue
      # maxmin
      g2=solve_maxmin(...); if improve update...
      # if none, perturb or break
      no_imp+=1
```

Function `upsample_sequence(p, n_new)`:
```
old_n=len(p)
if n_new <= old_n: return p
x_old=np.arange(old_n, dtype=float)
x_new=(np.arange(n_new)+0.5)* (old_n-1) / n_new
y=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
y=np.maximum(y,0); s=y.sum(); if s>0: y/=s
return y
```

`convolve_fast`:
```
def convolve_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([0.0])
    if n <= 1024:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
For normalized p, c sum ~1. Roundoff small.

`score_normalized` maybe use direct formula for speed:
```
def score_of(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).ravel()
    if p.size==0: return np.inf
    s=p.sum()
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    c=convolve_fast(p)
    mb=c.max()
    if not np.isfinite(mb): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*p.size*mb/(s*s))
```
Could be called often; avoid c max if negative? okay.

`normalize_sequence` returns p sum1, clips.

`make_mixture_power`:
```
def make_mixture_power(a,b,logw,n):
    i = np.arange(n) + 0.5
    logi = np.log(i)
    l1 = a * logi
    l2 = b * logi + logw
    m = np.maximum(l1, l2)
    # if m not finite? handle
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(m)):
        # fallback simple
    val = np.exp(l1-m) + np.exp(l2-m)
    val = np.maximum(val,0.0)
    s = val.sum()
    if s <=0: val = np.ones(n)
    return val/s
```
If logw very negative (e.g. -100), l2-m maybe -100, exp 0. Good. If logw >100, l1-m underflow. Use clipping for exp? `np.exp` of negative large returns0; of positive? l1-m≤0, l2-m≤0 by max, so safe. If m = -inf? if a,b finite and i positive, logs finite, no.

Beta tilt:
```
def make_beta_tilt(a,b,n):
   i=np.arange(n)+0.5
   logp=a*np.log(i)+b*np.log(n+1.0-i)
   logp-=logp.max(); p=np.exp(logp); p/=p.sum(); return np.maximum(p,0)
```
If b negative, n+1-i from n+0.5 down0.5; finite.

Now, LP precompute positions only if HAVE_SCIPY and time. For n=512:
```
def precompute_rows_cols(n):
   ar=np.arange(n,dtype=np.int32)
   Ri, Gj = np.meshgrid(ar, ar, indexing='ij')
   rows=(Ri+Gj).ravel().astype(np.int32)
   cols=Gj.ravel().astype(np.int32)
   return rows, cols
```
This Ri/Gj memory okay. Could avoid meshgrid by using `rows = (np.arange(n)[:,None] + np.arange(n)[None,:]).ravel().astype(np.int32)`; cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n),n). This creates 2D arrays too. Fine.

LP solve maxsum:
```
def solve_lp_maxsum(p, n, rows, cols, M, tlimit=5.0):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or M <=0: return None
    data = np.repeat(p, n)
    A = sp_sparse.coo_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    c = -np.ones(n)
    b = np.full(2*n-1, M)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
    try:
       res = linprog(c, A_ub=A.tocsr(), b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': tlimit})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x') or res.status not in (0,): return None
    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    s = g.sum()
    if s <= 1e-14: return None
    g /= s
    return g
```
Could maxsum objective unbounded if p has tiny entries; bounds no upper. We can add upper bound `max_bound = max(1e6, 100 * M / max(p.min(),1e-12), 10.0/(n))`? But if cap too low, no. Use upper=100.0 maybe? For normalized, an entry >100 impossible? sum1 so >100 dominates; not useful. But cap prevents unbounded. Add `upper = max(10.0, 100.0 * M / max(p[0], 1e-12), 1e-3)`? Actually p0 ~0.04, M~0.002, M/p0~0.05. But g normalized can have max maybe up to ~10? If cap 10 safe. Since sum1, max entry could be 1 (concentrated), but that would likely violate constraints. Cap at 10.0 (greater than any reasonable probability) safe. Use bounds=[(0.0,10.0)]*n. If true feasible needs >10, score would be bad; not needed. This bounds LP and numerical.

Maxmin LP:
```
def solve_lp_maxmin(p,n,rows,cols,M,tlimit=5.0):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
   # A_p coo as above data repeat p
   # extra rows
   extra_rows = np.arange(2*n-1, 2*n-1+n, dtype=np.int32)
   # for each j: -g_j + z <=0: two entries
   extra_cols = np.empty(2*n, dtype=np.int32)
   extra_data = np.empty(2*n, dtype=np.float64)
   extra_cols[0::2] = np.arange(n)
   extra_cols[1::2] = n
   extra_rows_rep = np.repeat(extra_rows, 2)
   extra_data[0::2] = -1.0
   extra_data[1::2] = 1.0
   data = np.concatenate((np.repeat(p,n), extra_data))
   rows_all = np.concatenate((rows, extra_rows_rep))
   cols_all = np.concatenate((cols, extra_cols))
   A = sp_sparse.coo_matrix((data,(rows_all,cols_all)), shape=(2*n-1+n, n+1), dtype=np.float64)
   c = np.zeros(n+1); c[n] = -1.0
   b_ub = np.concatenate((np.full(2*n-1, M), np.zeros(n)))
   A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
   b_eq = np.array([1.0])
   bounds = [(0.0,1.0)]*n + [(0.0,1.0)]  # g probabilities, z in [0,1]
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A.tocsr(), b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit':tlimit})
   ...
```
Since g sum=1 and nonnegative, entries <=1, z<=1. Good.
If res.status 0, g=res.x[:n]; normalize; return.

The maxmin LP may be slower due equality. Use only if maxsum no improve and time enough.

Line search function:
```
def line_search_direction(p, g, current_score, n):
    best_p=p.copy(); best_s=current_score; best_a=0.0
    # ensure g normalized
    alphas = [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]
    for a in alphas:
       pt = (1.0-a)*p + a*g
       pt = normalize_sequence(pt)
       if pt is None: continue
       s = score_of(pt)
       if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s:
           best_s=s; best_p=pt; best_a=a
    return best_p,best_s,best_a
```
If current_score not equal score(p) (if p perturbed), use score(p) first. In LP, p is best, current=best_score.

LP optimize:
```
def lp_optimize(p0,n,rows,cols,deadline):
    p=normalize_sequence(p0); if p None return p0,best_score
    best=p.copy(); best_s=score_of(p)
    no_improve=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and no_improve < 15:
        c=convolve_fast(p); M=c.max()
        if M <= 0: break
        score=2*n*M
        if score < 2*n/(2*n-1)*(1+1e-5): break
        tlimit=min(8.0, max(1.0, (deadline - time.monotonic())*0.1))
        improved=False
        g=solve_lp_maxsum(..., tlimit)
        if g is not None:
           p2,s2,a=line_search_direction(p,g,best_s,n)
           if s2 < best_s*(1-1e-8):
              p=p2; best=p.copy(); best_s=s2; no_improve=0; improved=True; print; it+=1; continue
        # maxmin
        g=solve_lp_maxmin(...)
        if g is not None:
           p2,s2,a=line_search...
           if improve: update
        if not improved:
           # small random direction
           noise_scale=0.05
           pn=p*(1.0 + noise_scale*(2*rng.random(n)-1))
           pn=normalize_sequence(pn)
           if pn is not None:
              sn=score_of(pn)
              if sn < best_s:
                  p=pn; best=pn; best_s=sn; no_improve=0; continue
           no_improve += 1
           it += 1
    return best,best_s
```
Need `rng` in lp; pass global rng or create inside top-level. No closures.

Let's write mirror more simply:
```
def mirror_optimize(p0,n,deadline,rng):
    p=normalize_sequence(p0); best=...; best_s=score_of(p)
    tau_factor=5.0
    step_size=0.1
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time < deadline and no_improve < 200:
       c=convolve_fast(p); M=c.max(); score=2*n*M
       if score < best_s:
          best=p.copy(); best_s=score; no_improve=0; step_size=max(0.02, step_size*0.995); tau_factor=max(0.2,tau_factor*0.998)
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       if no_improve > 80:
          break
       avg=1.0/(2*n-1)
       tau=tau_factor*max(M, avg)
       shifted=(c-M)/tau
       shifted=np.clip(shifted, -20.0, 0.0)
       w=np.exp(shifted); sw=w.sum()
       if sw<=0 or not finite:
          w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1.0
       else: w/=sw
       # correlation
       L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
       Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L); Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
       gp=2.0*np.fft.irfft(np.conj(Fp)*Fw,L)[:n].real
       gp=np.maximum(gp,0.0)
       md=gp.max()
       if md <=0: break
       update=(step_size/md)*gp
       update=np.clip(update, 0.0, step_size*2.0)
       p_try=p*np.exp(-update)
       acc=False
       for frac in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125]:
          pt=normalize_sequence(p*np.exp(-update*frac))
          if pt is None: continue
          st=score_of(pt)
          if st < best_s*(1-1e-9):
              p=pt; acc=True; break
       if acc:
          # continue
          pass
       else:
          # If many no improve, perturb
          if no_improve % 10 == 0:
             pt=p*(1.0+0.05*rng.normal(size=n)); pt=normalize(pt)
             if pt is not None and score_of(pt)<best_s: p=pt
       it+=1
    return best,best_s
```
This may be okay.

Random polish:
```
def random_polish(p0,n,deadline,rng):
   p=normalize(p0); best=...; no=0
   while time<deadline and no<100:
      width = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//5)))
      start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
      cand=p.copy()
      logmult = rng.normal(0.0, 0.15, width)
      cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(logmult)
      if rng.random()<0.3:
         cand = 0.5*(cand + cand[::-1])
      cand=normalize(cand)
      sc=score(cand)
      if finite and sc<best_s:
         best=cand; best_s=sc; no=0
      else: no+=1
   return best,best_s
```
Symmetrizing may hurt because symmetric sequences score≥2; don't symmetrize power law! Remove symmetry. Or only slight? Do not symmetrize. Random block multiplicative can adjust asym.

Upsample function and final selection.

Let's consider if mirror gradient with LSE weights and correlation `gp` uses p_j w_{i+j}. The loss LSE = log(Σ exp(c_k/τ)). c_k = Σ_{i+j=k} p_i p_j. ∂c_k/∂p_i = 2 p_{k-i}. Thus ∂L/∂p_i = Σ_k v_k 2 p_{k-i} = 2 Σ_j v_{i+j} p_j. Yes `gp`. We then do natural gradient in p: p ← p exp(-η gp). This is mirror descent for p with KL. Good.

But p variables have constraint sum=1; natural gradient should be p*(gp - Σ p gp) maybe? Multiplicative update without subtracting mean changes sum; we renormalize. For KL mirror descent on simplex, update p_i ← p_i exp(-η gp_i)/Z. This does not subtract. Renormalization effectively subtracts mean. Good.

In softmax Adam, subtracting mean; in mirror, renormalize. Good.

Could `gp` be large for small p? If p_j large and w aligns, update can zero some p. Clip? We can floor p after normalization: p = np.maximum(p, 1e-15); p/=p.sum(). But flooring changes sum slightly; okay. In normalize_sequence, don't floor too high. Use `p = np.clip(p, 1e-300, None)`; if extreme, score may be inf? fine.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    score = 2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The program uses:
1) a broad grid of one-sided power-law / mixture densities,
2) linear-programming peak-shaving directions (inspired by the SOTA LP step),
3) smooth log-sum-exp mirror-descent polishing,
4) random block polishing,
5) interpolation to longer lengths when it improves the score.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    import scipy.sparse as sp_sparse
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


def normalize_sequence(a):
    """Convert to a non-negative 1D float array with sum 1, or None."""
    try:
        p = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    p = p / s
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    return p


def convolve_fast(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a (default np.convolve behavior)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 1024:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    L = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    return c[:2 * n - 1]


def score_of(a):
    """Fast internal score matching evaluate_sequence for valid non-negative input."""
    p = normalize_sequence(a)
    if p is None:
        return np.inf
    n = p.size
    c = convolve_fast(p)
    mb = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mb)


def exact_score(seq):
    """Use the external evaluator if available; otherwise fall back internally."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)  # noqa: F821  (assumed imported by judge)
        val = float(val)
        if np.isfinite(val):
            return val
    except Exception:
        pass
    return score_of(seq)


def make_mixture_power(a, b, logw, n):
    """
    Sequence proportional to
        x^a + exp(logw) * x^b,  x=(i+0.5)/n up to scale.
    """
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5
    logi = np.log(i)
    l1 = a * logi
    l2 = b * logi + logw
    m = np.maximum(l1, l2)
    val = np.exp(l1 - m) + np.exp(l2 - m)
    val = np.where(np.isfinite(val), val, 0.0)
    val = np.maximum(val, 0.0)
    s = float(val.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        val = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(val.sum())
    return val / s


def make_beta_tilt(a, b, n):
    """
    Sequence proportional to x^a * (1-x)^b (discretized), with x ~ i/n.
    """
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5
    logp = a * np.log(i) + b * np.log((n + 0.5) - i)
    logp = np.where(np.isfinite(logp), logp, -1e300)
    logp -= np.max(logp)
    p = np.exp(logp)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(p.sum())
    return p / s


def add_candidate(cands, scores, p):
    """Add a valid normalized candidate and its score."""
    p = normalize_sequence(p)
    if p is None:
        return
    s = score_of(p)
    if np.isfinite(s):
        cands.append(p)
        scores.append(float(s))


def generate_candidates(n, deadline):
    """Generate a diverse set of good initial candidates."""
    cands = []
    scores = []

    # Uniform.
    add_candidate(cands, scores, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n)

    # Important one-sided singular density.
    add_candidate(cands, scores, make_mixture_power(-0.5, 0.0, -50.0, n))

    # Mixture of two powers: x^a + w x^b.
    a_vals = [-0.55, -0.5, -0.45, -0.4, -0.3, -0.2, -0.1, 0.0, 0.2]
    b_vals = [0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0]
    logw_vals = [-5.0, -3.0, -1.0, 0.0, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0]

    for a in a_vals:
        for b in b_vals:
            for lw in logw_vals:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                    break
                add_candidate(cands, scores, make_mixture_power(a, b, lw, n))
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

    # Beta-tilt family: x^a (1-x)^b.
    ab_vals = [-0.55, -0.5, -0.45, -0.4, -0.3, -0.2, 0.0]
    bb_vals = [-0.3, -0.1, 0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.6]
    for a in ab_vals:
        for b in bb_vals:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            add_candidate(cands, scores, make_beta_tilt(a, b, n))
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

    # If a previous construction is available, resample it and include it.
    try:
        _h = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
        if _h is not None:
            ph = normalize_sequence(_h)
            if ph is not None:
                if ph.size != n:
                    ph = upsample_sequence(ph, n)
                add_candidate(cands, scores, ph)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if not cands:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
        cands.append(p)
        scores.append(score_of(p))

    order = np.argsort(scores)
    top = []
    for idx in order[:12]:
        top.append((float(scores[idx]), cands[int(idx)]))
    return top


def precompute_rows_cols(n):
    """
    Precompute sparse coordinates for constraints convolve(p, q)_k = sum_j p_i q_j.
    Rows are k = i+j, columns are j, data will be p_i.
    """
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32)
    Ri, Gj = np.meshgrid(ar, ar, indexing="ij")
    rows = (Ri + Gj).ravel().astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    cols = Gj.ravel().astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    return rows, cols


def safe_lp_p(p, lower=1e-10):
    """Add a tiny floor so LPs are numerically bounded."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.maximum(p, lower)
    p /= p.sum()
    return p


def solve_lp_maxsum(p, n, rows, cols, M, tlimit=5.0):
    """
    Maximize sum(q) subject to p*q <= M, q >= 0.
    Returns normalized q (sum 1) or None.
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    if M <= 0.0:
        return None

    try:
        data = np.repeat(p, n)
        A = sp_sparse.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"time_limit": float(tlimit)}
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or not hasattr(res, "x"):
        return None
    if int(getattr(res, "status", -1)) != 0:
        return None

    q = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.5:
        return None
    q /= s
    return q


def solve_lp_maxmin(p, n, rows, cols, M, tlimit=5.0):
    """
    Maximize z subject to q >= z >= 0, sum(q)=1, p*q <= M.
    This produces a normalized direction that is as "even" as possible.
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    if M <= 0.0:
        return None

    try:
        data_base = np.repeat(p, n)

        extra_rows = np.arange(2 * n - 1, 2 * n - 1 + n, dtype=np.int32)
        extra_rows_rep = np.repeat(extra_rows, 2)

        extra_cols = np.empty(2 * n, dtype=np.int32)
        extra_cols[0::2] = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32)
        extra_cols[1::2] = n

        extra_data = np.empty(2 * n, dtype=np.float64)
        extra_data[0::2] = -1.0
        extra_data[1::2] = 1.0

        data = np.concatenate((data_base, extra_data))
        rows_all = np.concatenate((rows, extra_rows_rep))
        cols_all = np.concatenate((cols, extra_cols))

        A = sp_sparse.csr_matrix(
            (data, (rows_all, cols_all)),
            shape=(3 * n - 1, n + 1),
            dtype=np.float64
        )

        c = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        c[n] = -1.0

        b_ub = np.concatenate((np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=np.float64),
                               np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)))

        A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
        A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
        b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

        bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n + [(0.0, 1.0)]

        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            A_eq=A_eq,
            b_eq=b_eq,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"time_limit": float(tlimit)}
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or not hasattr(res, "x"):
        return None
    if int(getattr(res, "status", -1)) != 0:
        return None

    q = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()[:n]
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    q /= s
    return q


def line_search_direction(p, q, current_score, n):
    """Try convex combinations p + alpha(q-p) and keep the best."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_s = current_score

    alphas = [0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0]
    for alpha in alphas:
        pt = (1.0 - alpha) * p + alpha * q
        pt = normalize_sequence(pt)
        if pt is None:
            continue
        st = score_of(pt)
        if np.isfinite(st) and st < best_s:
            best_s = st
            best_p = pt

    return best_p, best_s


def lp_optimize(p0, n, rows, cols, deadline, rng):
    """Iterative LP peak-shaving."""
    p = normalize_sequence(p0)
    if p is None:
        return p0, score_of(p0)

    best = p.copy()
    best_s = score_of(p)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    lower_bound = 2.0 * n / (2.0 * n - 1.0)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and no_improve < 12:
        c = convolve_fast(p)
        M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
            break
        cur_s = 2.0 * n * M
        if cur_s < lower_bound * (1.0 + 1e-5):
            break

        remain = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remain < 5.0:
            break
        tlimit = min(8.0, max(1.0, 0.15 * remain))

        improved = False
        p_safe = safe_lp_p(p, lower=1e-10)

        q = solve_lp_maxsum(p_safe, n, rows, cols, M, tlimit)
        if q is not None:
            p2, s2 = line_search_direction(p, q, best_s, n)
            if s2 < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-9):
                p = p2
                best = p2.copy()
                best_s = s2
                no_improve = 0
                improved = True
                print(f"LP maxsum iter {it}: score={best_s:.6f}", flush=True)
            it += 1
            if improved:
                continue

        q = solve_lp_maxmin(p_safe, n, rows, cols, M, tlimit)
        if q is not None:
            p2, s2 = line_search_direction(p, q, best_s, n)
            if s2 < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-9):
                p = p2
                best = p2.copy()
                best_s = s2
                no_improve = 0
                improved = True
                print(f"LP maxmin iter {it}: score={best_s:.6f}", flush=True)
            it += 1

        if not improved:
            # Small stochastic lift to escape a local basin.
            noise = 0.05 * (2.0 * rng.random(n) - 1.0)
            pn = p * (1.0 + noise)
            pn = normalize_sequence(pn)
            if pn is not None:
                sn = score_of(pn)
                if np.isfinite(sn) and sn < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-9):
                    p = pn
                    best = pn.copy()
                    best_s = sn
                    no_improve = 0
                    print(f"LP random lift iter {it}: score={best_s:.6f}", flush=True)
                    continue
            no_improve += 1

    return best, best_s


def correlation_for_update(p, weights):
    """
    Compute g_i = 2 * sum_j p_j * weights_{i+j} for i=0..n-1.
    This is the gradient of a log-sum-exp objective wrt p.
    """
    n = p.size
    m = weights.size
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 1 << (n + m - 1).bit_length()
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(weights, L)
    g = 2.0 * np.fft.irfft(np.conj(Fp) * Fw, L)[:n]
    return np.maximum(g, 0.0)


def mirror_optimize(p0, n, deadline, rng):
    """
    Mirror descent on the simplex with a smooth peak objective.
    Minimizes approximately max_k conv(p,p)_k.
    """
    p = normalize_sequence(p0)
    if p is None:
        return p0, score_of(p0)

    best = p.copy()
    best_s = score_of(p)
    tau_factor = 5.0
    step_size = 0.12
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    avg = 1.0 / (2.0 * n - 1.0)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and no_improve < 250:
        c = convolve_fast(p)
        M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
            break
        s = 2.0 * n * M
        if s < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-10):
            best = p.copy()
            best_s = s
            no_improve = 0
            step_size = max(0.02, step_size * 0.998)
            tau_factor = max(0.2, tau_factor * 0.997)
            if it % 50 == 0:
                print(f"mirror iter {it}: score={best_s:.6f}", flush=True)
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 120:
            # Occasionally perturb to escape.
            if no_improve % 20 == 0:
                pt = p * (1.0 + 0.04 * rng.normal(size=n))
                pt = normalize_sequence(pt)
                if pt is not None:
                    st = score_of(pt)
                    if np.isfinite(st) and st < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-10):
                        p = pt
                        continue
            if no_improve > 220:
                break

        tau = max(tau_factor * max(M, avg), 1e-12)
        shifted = (c - M) / tau
        shifted = np.clip(shifted, -20.0, 0.0)
        w = np.exp(shifted)
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
            w = np.zeros_like(c)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
            sw = 1.0
        w = w / sw

        gp = correlation_for_update(p, w)
        md = float(gp.max()) if gp.size else 0.0
        if md <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(md):
            break

        update_base = (step_size / md) * gp
        update_base = np.clip(update_base, 0.0, 2.0 * step_size)

        accepted = False
        for frac in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
            log_factor = np.clip(-update_base * frac, -8.0, 0.0)
            pt = p * np.exp(log_factor)
            pt = normalize_sequence(pt)
            if pt is None:
                continue
            st = score_of(pt)
            if np.isfinite(st) and st < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-10):
                p = pt
                accepted = True
                break

        if not accepted:
            # If updates keep failing, reduce step size.
            step_size = max(0.02, step_size * 0.9)

        it += 1

    return best, best_s


def random_polish(p0, n, deadline, rng):
    """Light stochastic block-multiplicative polish."""
    p = normalize_sequence(p0)
    if p is None:
        return p0, score_of(p0)

    best = p.copy()
    best_s = score_of(p)
    no_improve = 0
    low_w = max(1, n // 400)
    high_w = max(2, n // 8)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and no_improve < 80:
        width = int(rng.integers(low_w, high_w + 1))
        width = min(width, n)
        if width <= 0:
            break
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        cand = p.copy()
        cand[start:start + width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, size=width))
        cand = normalize_sequence(cand)
        if cand is None:
            no_improve += 1
            continue
        sc = score_of(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-10):
            best = cand
            best_s = sc
            no_improve = 0
            if no_improve == 0 and int(best_s * 1000) % 2 == 0:
                print(f"random polish: score={best_s:.6f}", flush=True)
        else:
            no_improve += 1

    return best, best_s


def upsample_sequence(p, n_new):
    """Linearly interpolate a probability sequence to a longer length."""
    p = normalize_sequence(p)
    if p is None:
        return None
    old_n = p.size
    if n_new <= 0:
        return None
    if n_new == old_n:
        return p
    x_old = np.arange(old_n, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = (np.arange(n_new, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) * (old_n - 1.0) / float(n_new)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
    y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        y = np.ones(n_new, dtype=np.float64) / n_new
    else:
        y /= s
    return y


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search driver.  Returns a Python list of non-negative floats.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)
    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = start + total_budget - 25.0

    # Search size: 512 gives a good speed/accuracy trade-off for LPs.
    n_search = 512

    # Phase 1: construct seeds.
    cand_deadline = min(deadline - 600.0, start + 180.0)
    top = generate_candidates(n_search, cand_deadline)
    if not top:
        p = np.ones(n_search, dtype=np.float64) / n_search
        top = [(score_of(p), p)]

    best_score, best_p = top[0]
    print(f"initial best score={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Phase 2: LP peak shaving from the best seed (often the biggest gain).
    if HAVE_SCIPY and time.monotonic() < deadline - 350.0:
        try:
            rows, cols = precompute_rows_cols(n_search)
            lp_deadline = min(deadline - 300.0, time.monotonic() + max(250.0, 0.55 * (deadline - time.monotonic())))
            p_lp, s_lp = lp_optimize(best_p, n_search, rows, cols, lp_deadline, rng)
            if p_lp is not None and np.isfinite(s_lp) and s_lp < best_score:
                best_p = p_lp
                best_score = float(s_lp)
                print(f"after LP: score={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"LP stage skipped/failed: {e}", flush=True)

    # Phase 3: mirror-descent polishing.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 180.0:
        mirror_deadline = min(deadline - 120.0, time.monotonic() + 300.0)
        try:
            p_m, s_m = mirror_optimize(best_p, n_search, mirror_deadline, rng)
            if p_m is not None and np.isfinite(s_m) and s_m < best_score:
                best_p = p_m
                best_score = float(s_m)
                print(f"after mirror: score={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"mirror stage skipped/failed: {e}", flush=True)

    # Phase 4: cheap random polish until a safety margin remains.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 35.0:
        try:
            p_r, s_r = random_polish(best_p, n_search, deadline - 35.0, rng)
            if p_r is not None and np.isfinite(s_r) and s_r < best_score:
                best_p = p_r
                best_score = float(s_r)
                print(f"after random polish: score={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"random polish skipped/failed: {e}", flush=True)

    # Phase 5: try longer interpolated versions.  Longer length often improves
    # the approximation to the continuous optimum, provided the evaluator is
    # still fast enough for the submitted length.
    final_candidates = []
    final_candidates.append((float(best_score), best_p))

    # Also consider interpolating the best few seeds.
    for sc, pc in top[:6]:
        final_candidates.append((float(sc), pc))

    target_lengths = []
    unique_best = [len(x) for _, x in final_candidates]
    for nl in (1024, 2048):
        if nl not in unique_best:
            target_lengths.append(nl)

    for _, pc in final_candidates[:8]:
        for nl in target_lengths:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 80.0:
                break
            pu = upsample_sequence(pc, nl)
            if pu is None:
                continue
            su = score_of(pu)
            if np.isfinite(su):
                final_candidates.append((float(su), pu))

    if len(final_candidates) > 30:
        final_candidates = sorted(final_candidates, key=lambda x: x[0])[:30]

    # Exact validation of the few best internal candidates.
    final_candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    validated = []
    for sc_int, seq in final_candidates[:10]:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20.0:
            break
        seq = np.maximum(np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
        seq = normalize_sequence(seq)
        if seq is None:
            continue
        sc_ex = exact_score(seq.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(sc_ex):
            validated.append((float(sc_ex), seq))

    if validated:
        validated.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
        best_score, best_p = validated[0]
    else:
        # Fallback: use the best internal one, but keep it valid.
        best_p = normalize_sequence(best_p)
        if best_p is None:
            best_p = np.ones(n_search, dtype=np.float64) / n_search
        best_score = score_of(best_p)

    # Optional exact score check for the chosen one.
    cross = exact_score([float(x) for x in best_p])
    if np.isfinite(cross) and cross < best_score:
        best_score = float(cross)

    final_seq = normalize_sequence(best_p)
    if final_seq is None:
        final_seq = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512

    final_seq = np.asarray(final_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    final_seq = np.where(np.isfinite(final_seq), final_seq, 0.0)
    final_seq = np.maximum(final_seq, 0.0)
    final_seq = final_seq / final_seq.sum()

    print(f"final selected score={best_score:.6f} n={final_seq.size}", flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in final_seq]
```
